0 · Agent pre-review (gemini-3.8-flash, 2026-09-25 — recommendation only; the board decides)

Recommendation: accredit with conditions · confidence high · your time if you follow the list: ≈ 32 min

This package is an advanced, highly specialized continuing professional education course on the endocrinology and peptide pharmacology of skin, developed for dermatologists, aesthetic clinicians, nurses, pharmacists, and allied medical professionals. It covers cutaneous neuro-immuno-endocrinology, the dermatologic effects of systemic hormones, facial structural changes from GLP-1/GIP agonists ('Ozempic face'), cosmetic signal peptides, and investigational compounds such as GHK-Cu, BPC-157, Melanotan II, and growth hormone secretagogues. Across seven instructional modules, the content rigorously disentangles biological mechanism, human clinical evidence (via GRADE), regulatory classification across six jurisdictions (US, GB, EU, PT, BR, AE), and clinical safety. The tone is sober, scientifically objective, strictly non-prescriptive, and consistently distinguishes clinical facts from commercial marketing claims.

Conditions.

  • Verify or correct the epidemiological citation in claim c-m02-19 regarding the 0.2% prevalence and >50% malignancy rate of androgen-secreting neoplasms.
  • Execute and verify the visual-qa automated pipeline for all 7 module video openers to ensure visual manifest compliance and avatar disclosure accuracy.
  • Perform manual browser verification of the negative registry assertions in the UAE EDE, ANVISA, and INFARMED portals where automated scrapers were restricted by portal technical architectures.
  • Ensure learner enrollment pages clearly reiterate that the credential awarded is a Geneva College of Longevity Science Certificate of Completion and that no CME/CE credits are currently conferred.

  2. c-m02-19 — The assertion that androgen-secreting neoplasms occur in 'about 0.2% of hyperandrogenic women; over half malignant' was searched in full in the cited Martin 2018 guideline abstract and A&RE guide and returned not_found. Verify the primary epidemiological source. (~5 min)

m5-p04, m5-p05, m5-p06		The Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology guide's first-pass hair-loss workup: patterned thinning → androgenetic alopecia (topical minoxidil; finasteride in men; treat coexisting iron or thyroid problems); diffuse shedding after a trigger → telogen effluvium (remove the trigger, reassure, recheck ferritin and TSH); patchy smooth loss (± nail pitting) → alopecia areata (dermatology referral; screen thyroid and autoimmune disease); signs of androgen excess → hyperandrogenism work-up; always ferritin and TSH, androgens if virilising, biopsy a scarring pattern; refer severe or rapidly progressive alopecia areata promptly.Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide

c-m05-09
m5-p08, m5-p09		Afamelanotide is an MC1R agonist. In two sponsor-funded phase 3 RCTs in erythropoietic protoporphyria it increased pain-free time in direct sunlight (US trial, n=94: median 69.4 vs 40.8 hours over six months; EU trial, n=74: 6.0 vs 0.8 hours over nine months, with fewer phototoxic reactions). Both guides grade it A for EPP. Use outside EPP remains investigational (for example a phase 3 vitiligo trial started in 2023 with no results posted).Afamelanotide for Erythropoietic Protoporphyria
N Engl J Med · 2015;373:48–59

c-m05-11
m5-p08		Melanotan II is a non-selective melanocortin agonist (MC1R, MC3R, MC4R, MC5R) that crosses into the brain. Its only controlled human data are a 1996 single-blind pilot in three men (tanning in two; nausea, yawning, spontaneous erections, somnolence) and a 1998 crossover in ten men with psychogenic erectile dysfunction; no trial has tested cosmetic tanning. The Peptide Pocket Guide grades it C and the Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology guide D ('harms > benefit'); the course grades cosmetic use D.Evaluation of melanotan-II, a superpotent cyclic melanotropic peptide in a pilot phase-I clinical study

m6-p02, m6-p04		Growth hormone acts mainly in the dermis: its receptor is expressed more by dermal fibroblasts than keratinocytes, and GH increases fibroblast numbers and supports collagen synthesis. IGF-1, made locally by fibroblasts and melanocytes, acts on keratinocytes through the IGF-1 receptor; in culture IGF-1, not GH, drives keratinocyte proliferation.Expression of growth hormone receptor, insulin-like growth factor 1 (IGF-1) and IGF-1 receptor mRNA and proteins in human skin

c-m07-02

3 · Scope, audience, jurisdictions

Professional scope. Continuing professional education for licensed clinicians and allied providers. Every learner gets the same evidence, regulatory status and safety content; what each may do with it — prescribe, recommend, administer, or refer — follows their own licence and scope, and the course adapts its practice guidance to that role. It informs clinical reasoning; it is not a protocol, contains no dosing, and does not replace specialist input, formal guidelines or individual judgment.

It informs clinical reasoning; it is not a protocol, contains no dosing, and does not replace specialist input, formal guidelines or individual judgment.

Personas. dermatologist, aesthetic physician or plastic surgeon evaluating hormone and peptide claims for skin · nurse practitioner or physician associate who prescribes or counsels in aesthetic, dermatology or primary care · registered nurse, aesthetic nurse or injector who delivers treatments and fields patient questions · other licensed providers (pharmacists, naturopathic doctors, allied aesthetic professionals) who advise on skin products and referrals

4 · Modules (7) — what the learner actually gets

M01 The skin as an endocrine organ, and how it ages · The skin as an endocrine organ, and how it ages · 29 min · 20 blocks

Objective. By the end, you can analyze the autonomous neuro-immuno-endocrine physiology of the skin and evaluate how local steroidogenesis, receptor expression, and cellular senescence drive structural dermal aging.

Agent: [none] Covers cutaneous neuro-immuno-endocrinology, autonomous steroidogenesis, the 2026 Hormone Cell Atlas, and cellular senescence (p16/SASP). Academically sound and carefully balanced.

BlockTypeWhat the learner hears / readsEvidence
m1-p01titleThe skin makes its own hormones.
This course starts with physiology, because every later question about a hormone or peptide for skin rests on it. Skin senses hormones from the blood, makes several of its own, and ages in ways that change both. In three lessons you will separate what happens locally in the skin from what the rest of the body sends in, and label each finding by the kind of evidence behind it.
—

m1-p02reflectionHow will you use this course?
The evidence and grades are the same for everyone, and regulatory notes follow the country you choose. What changes with your role, in every module, is the 'what you do next' at the end of each lesson, so it fits what your licence lets you do.
—
m1-p03audioFour units, one circuit.
Think of the skin as a sensing organ with its own hormone supply. Researchers who study it describe four units, epidermal, dermal, hypodermal and adnexal, the last meaning hair follicles and glands. Each unit holds cells that can make hormones and mount an immune response, such as keratinocytes, fibroblasts, sebocytes, fat cells and mast cells. Signals move three ways. Neighbouring cells talk directly, sensory nerves carry messages in and out, and some products enter the blood and reach distant organs. Keep one distinction in view for the whole module. A signal that is made and used inside the skin is a local loop. A hormone that arrives from the blood, or leaves the skin for the rest of the body, is systemic signalling. Much of what follows is mechanism, drawn from cell, tissue and animal studies, and each finding will be labelled that way.
Neuro-immuno-endocrinology of the skin: how environment regulates body homeostasis
Neuroendocrinology of the skin
The human skin as a hormone target and an endocrine gland
Neuronal control of skin function: the skin as a neuroimmunoendocrine organ
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m1-p04toolA steroid factory with local axes.
Skin cells carry the enzymes to build steroids from cholesterol or to activate precursors arriving in the blood, and they run shortened copies of the body's central hormone axes. Each row names the evidence it rests on, because most of it comes from cells, tissue kept alive outside the body, or mice.

m1-p05toolThree directions of traffic.
Hormone signals in skin run in three directions. Some are made and used in the same patch of skin. Some arrive from the blood. A few leave the skin and act on the rest of the body. Knowing which one you are looking at decides whether the answer lies in the skin or in a systemic assessment.

m1-p06audioRead the skin before the serum.
Picture the consultation where a patient asks about thinning, bruising skin. The Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide asks you to read that skin as a window on the whole endocrine system before reaching for a serum. Sudden thinning with easy bruising suggests cortisol excess. Coarse, dry, cool skin suggests an underactive thyroid. New acanthosis nigricans points to insulin resistance. The guide lists violaceous striae with new hypertension or diabetes, rapidly progressive virilisation, and signs of an endocrine tumour as reasons to refer promptly to endocrinology. The local loops from the last two moments explain how skin behaves day to day. They are foundation biology, and the guide is explicit that they are no rationale for dosing anyone with a hormone. Whatever your role, a systemic pattern in the skin gets a systemic answer.

m1-p07quiz'My skin makes its own cortisol.'
One question to close lesson 1.1.

Lesson 1.1
m1-p08audioA map of fourteen million cells.
Fourteen million cells is the scale of the Hormone Cell Atlas, a Cambridge-led study published in Science in 2026. The team read the RNA for 379 hormone and receptor genes in single cells and nuclei from forty-seven human tissues, skin included. From those readings they scored each cell type for its predicted capacity to make or to receive a hormone, and they flagged unexpected sites, such as RNA for the gut hormone secretin in a type of immune cell. The Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide uses the atlas to argue that the skin, built from every major cell lineage, is plausibly one of the body's largest distributed signalling networks. The word plausibly matters. Everything in the atlas starts from RNA, and RNA tells you what a cell could make. Whether the cell actually makes and releases the hormone is the question the rest of this lesson answers.
A Hormone Cell Atlas maps the human endocrine system at cellular resolution
Hormone Cell Atlas portal — About and FAQs
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m1-p09stat
379 hormone and receptor genes read as RNA, cell by cell		Prediction, in the authors' own words.
The atlas's own portal describes its scores as 'putative and hypothesis-generating'. A few predictions were tested in cells in the paper itself; the rest are left for users to validate. Across biology, the amount of RNA for a gene is often a poor guide to how much protein a cell makes, and protein is still a step short of secretion.
A Hormone Cell Atlas maps the human endocrine system at cellular resolution
Hormone Cell Atlas portal — About and FAQs
On the Dependency of Cellular Protein Levels on mRNA Abundance

m1-p11audioA map to confirm, then a trial.
Two habits follow from the atlas. The first concerns product claims. Topical and transdermal treatments act through receptors the skin already carries, and the skin's own enzymes change how strongly a hormone acts once it arrives, so the response in skin can differ from what the dose predicts. A receptor on a map is where an efficacy question starts, and the answer still needs a trial. The second habit concerns the physiology you think you know. The guide's testing pearl says serum vitamin D falls with sunscreen use, which the biology predicts. An independent review found that in real-life field trials, daily sunscreen did not lower blood levels, although the sunscreens tested were moderate and today's high-protection products have never been trialled. Keep recommending sun protection, and treat vitamin D as its own question.
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
The effect of sunscreen on vitamin D: a review
A Hormone Cell Atlas maps the human endocrine system at cellular resolution
Vitamin D metabolism, mechanism of action, and clinical applications
m1-p12quiz'Your skin has receptors for it.'
One question to close lesson 1.2.

Lesson 1.2
m1-p13audioCells that stop dividing and keep talking.
Senescence begins as a safety mechanism. A cell that has taken enough damage stops dividing for good, which helps guard against cancer and is useful during wound repair. The problem is what the cell does next. It stays alive and metabolically busy and releases a mixture of inflammatory cytokines such as interleukin six and eight, enzymes that break down matrix, and growth factors. This secretory profile, the SASP, was characterised in human cells grown in the laboratory after DNA damage. In living human skin, the evidence is observational. Skin samples from older people contain more cells carrying the senescence marker p16, in both the epidermis and the dermis, and fibroblasts, keratinocytes and melanocytes can all become senescent. Researchers also caution that no single marker identifies a senescent cell with certainty, so counts depend on the marker chosen.
Senescence-associated secretory phenotypes reveal cell-nonautonomous functions of oncogenic RAS and the p53 tumor suppressor
p16INK4A is a robust in vivo biomarker of cellular aging in human skin
Faces of cellular senescence in skin aging
Biomarkers of Cellular Senescence and Skin Aging
Targeting cellular senescence in dermatology: senolytic and senomorphic strategies
Naturally occurring p16(Ink4a)-positive cells shorten healthy lifespan
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m1-p14stat
3 years younger-looking: fewest versus most p16-positive epidermal cells, at the same chronological age		Three years of perceived age.
In a Dutch cohort, people with more p16-positive cells in sun-protected arm skin had more ageing-type elastic fibres, more facial wrinkles and looked older, after allowing for their actual age. The epidermal p16-positive cells were mostly melanocytes. The study was publicly funded; one co-author worked for Unilever.
P16INK4a Positive Cells in Human Skin Are Indicative of Local Elastic Fiber Morphology, Facial Wrinkling, and Perceived Age
p16INK4A is a robust in vivo biomarker of cellular aging in human skin

m1-p16audioThe levers with trials behind them.
Set the senescence story beside the interventions that have been tested in people. In Nambour, Australia, a publicly funded trial randomised 903 adults to daily or discretionary sunscreen, and over four and a half years skin ageing was about a quarter lower with daily use. Topical tretinoin has a run of randomised trials in photoaged skin that agree with one another, and the Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide rates topical retinoids first-line, grade A. Retinol has smaller trials, including one in residents in their late eighties whose fine wrinkling improved and whose biopsies showed more procollagen. Both levers protect or rebuild the dermal matrix that ultraviolet light and, it is thought, the SASP erode, and both have human outcome data. Senolytics for skin have no such data yet. Keep that order in mind when a patient asks what actually works.

Topical tretinoin for treating photoaging: A systematic review of randomized controlled trials
Improvement of naturally aged skin with vitamin A (retinol)
Vitamin A antagonizes decreased cell growth and elevated collagen-degrading matrix metalloproteinases and stimulates collagen accumulation in naturally aged human skin
Mechanisms of photoaging and chronological skin aging

m1-p17audio'Can a senolytic fix my skin?'
Consider a fifty-eight-year-old patient, fictional and built from this lesson's evidence. She has read that senolytic supplements clear so-called zombie cells and reverse skin ageing, and she asks whether to start one she found online. She does not wear sunscreen every day. Three facts frame the conversation. Senescent cells really do accumulate in human skin, and their number tracks with wrinkles and with how old a person looks. The evidence that clearing them helps comes from mice, and the human studies so far are small pilots measuring markers in tissue. Daily sunscreen and topical retinoids, by contrast, have randomised trials with visible skin outcomes. The Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide advises keeping senolytics within clinical trials. Decide what you say first.

M02 Systemic hormones and the skin · Systemic hormones and the skin · 30 min · 20 blocks

Objective. By the end, you can differentiate cutaneous manifestations of systemic endocrine disorders from localized hormonal aging, appraising the clinical limitations and risks of hormone replacement in skin health.

m2-p01titleThe face that might be a diagnosis.
Most skin changes you see are ageing or lifestyle. A few are the first visible sign of endocrine disease. In three lessons you will separate them: what local and systemic hormones do to skin, what hormone therapy can and cannot do for it, and which signs belong with an endocrinologist.

m2-p02audioThe skin regenerates its own cortisol.
Picture the skin as a small endocrine organ with its own stress axis. Skin cells make CRH and ACTH, the same signals that drive the adrenal glands, and they carry the receptors to respond to them. They also carry an enzyme called eleven beta HSD one, which turns inactive cortisone back into active cortisol inside the cell. In human skin samples that enzyme works harder with age and in sun-exposed skin, and it sets local cortisol independently of the level in the blood. Cortisol slows collagen production in dermal fibroblasts, and mice bred without the enzyme kept their dermal thickness as they aged. Hold all of this in the mechanism register. It comes from biopsies, cultured cells and mice, and it says nothing yet about how a patient's face will age.
Neuroendocrinology of the skin

11β-Hydroxysteroid dehydrogenase blockade prevents age-induced skin structure and function defects

m2-p03tool11β-HSD1: four separate answers.

m2-p04toolWhich features point to Cushing's?
Cushing's syndrome shares most of its features with ordinary weight gain, poor sleep and stress. The Endocrine Society's diagnostic guideline separates the few that discriminate from the many that are common. Before you see its verdicts, sort these eight.

m2-p05stat
2–3 new cases of endogenous Cushing's syndrome per million people per year (European population studies)		Rare, and usually from a prescription.
Endogenous Cushing's syndrome is uncommon: European population studies put it at two to three new cases per million people a year. The commonest cause of Cushing's syndrome is iatrogenic, from glucocorticoids given as medicine. The Endocrine Society therefore asks for a drug history first, covering tablets, inhalers, injections and creams, including skin-bleaching products, and recommends testing when features are multiple and progressive.
The diagnosis of Cushing's syndrome: an Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m2-p06audioReassure the many, refer the few.
Here is how that plays out when a patient asks about cortisol face. Begin with a drug history, because the commonest cause of cortisol excess is a glucocorticoid someone is already using. The guideline names the easy ones to miss, such as skin-bleaching creams, herbal tonics, and joint or nerve injections. Then look for the four features that shift the odds, meaning easy bruising, a plethoric face, weakness in the hips and shoulders, and wide reddish-purple striae. Without them, the Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide reads most puffiness as sleep, alcohol, salt and fluid, and the Endocrine Society advises against testing everyone. With them, and especially when they are progressing, the patient needs formal testing and an endocrinologist. A cortisol supplement or a mail-order saliva panel answers the wrong question.
The diagnosis of Cushing's syndrome: an Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline

m2-p08audioEstrogen and the dermis.
Estrogen is the hormone the guide says dominates the visible ageing of women's skin, so start with what it does in the dermis. Skin carries estrogen receptors in sun-protected and sun-damaged sites alike. In a vehicle-controlled study, two weeks of topical estradiol raised collagen production in the hip skin of postmenopausal women; in photoaged forearm and facial skin, the same treatment made no measurable difference. Small biopsy studies from the nineteen eighties and nineties found that skin collagen and thickness fall after menopause, and that the fall follows years since the last period more closely than birthdays. Keep two questions apart as you go. One is what estrogen does in the dermis. The other is whether giving it changes how a face ages, and those answers come from different trials.
Induction of collagen by estradiol: difference between sun-protected and photodamaged human skin in vivo
Estrogens and aging skin
Long-term effects of the menopause and sex hormones on skin thickness
Effects of postmenopausal hypoestrogenism on skin collagen

m2-p09stat
1–2% fall in skin collagen content and thickness per year after menopause (small biopsy studies)		One to two per cent a year.
In small biopsy studies of postmenopausal women, skin collagen content and skin thickness fell by about 1–2% a year, in step with bone mass, and followed years since menopause more than age. Reviews, and the Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology guide, quote a steeper early loss of up to 30% in the first five years. That figure traces to the same small studies, and a check of their abstracts in September 2026 did not find it stated.

m2-p10toolBiopsy gains, mirror results.
Hormone therapy has two kinds of trial behind it. Small trials measure the dermis; larger trials measure the face. They point in different directions, so read them separately. This course grades the structural gains C and teaches the negative wrinkle trials alongside them.

m2-p11audioSymptoms and bone. Skin is a bystander.
Where does that leave hormone therapy for skin? In the United States, as of February 2026, the approved uses of menopausal hormone therapy are hot flashes, vaginal and vulvar symptoms, and for some products the prevention of bone loss. In late 2025 the FDA asked manufacturers to remove heart, breast cancer and dementia statements from the boxed warning, and it approved the first revised labels in February 2026. Skin still appears nowhere among the uses. The menopause society's 2022 position statement finds the balance favourable for symptoms and bone when therapy starts before sixty or within ten years of menopause, and it finds too little evidence for compounded bioidentical hormones. Low-dose vaginal estrogen for genitourinary symptoms is the one use aimed at the tissue itself. For the face, daily sunscreen and a prescription retinoid carry better evidence than any hormone. Treat symptoms on their merits, and count any skin change as a bonus.

Lesson 2.2
m2-p13audioSome syndromes show on the skin first.
Some endocrine syndromes are written on the skin years before the glands give them away. In multiple endocrine neoplasia type one, most patients in one careful series had several facial angiofibromas, the same lesions seen in tuberous sclerosis, along with collagenomas on the trunk. In Carney complex, usually caused by inactivating variants in the PRKAR1A gene, freckle-like lentigines cluster on the lips, eyelids and the corners of the eyes, alongside blue naevi and small skin myxomas. The same patients can develop myxomas in the heart and cortisol-secreting adrenal disease. In MEN two A, an itchy, rippled, darkened patch between the shoulder blades, called cutaneous lichen amyloidosis, can appear long before medullary thyroid cancer. Each of these is a referral finding, and none of them is yours to diagnose alone.

m2-p14stat
88% of 32 people with MEN1 had multiple facial angiofibromas		Eighty-eight per cent.
In a consecutive series of 32 people with MEN1 examined at the US National Institutes of Health, 88% had multiple facial angiofibromas and 72% had collagenomas. The angiofibromas were clinically and histologically identical to those of tuberous sclerosis. The authors concluded that angiofibromas in someone without tuberous sclerosis call for biochemical testing for MEN1.
Multiple facial angiofibromas and collagenomas in patients with multiple endocrine neoplasia type 1
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m2-p15audioThe everyday endocrine signs.
Most of the endocrine skin signs you will actually meet come from common conditions. New, generalised darkening, most obvious in skin creases such as the palms, in scars, on the gums and on sun-exposed skin, can be the sign of primary adrenal insufficiency. In a patient who is also unwell, the Endocrine Society recommends testing, and a suspected adrenal crisis is treated before the results come back, because delay raises the risk of death. Hirsutism that arrives quickly, keeps progressing, or comes with a deeper voice or clitoromegaly makes an androgen-secreting tumour more likely, and more than half of those tumours are malignant. Velvety dark plaques in the neck folds track insulin resistance. Firm plaques on the shins point to Graves' disease, and cold, dry, coarse skin suggests an underactive thyroid. The skin shows you which door to knock on.

m2-p17audioFreckles before the wedding.

M03 Fat, incretins and the ageing face · Fat, incretins and the ageing face · 31 min · 21 blocks

Objective. By the end, you can evaluate the pathophysiology of dermal white adipose tissue, systemic inflammaging, and the facial structural consequences of GLP-1 and dual-incretin receptor agonist therapies.

m3-p01titleThe face changes when the fat does.
Facial shape depends on fat as much as on collagen. In three lessons you will separate what is known about the skin's own fat, sugar damage to the dermis, weight-loss drugs and the gut from what is still mechanism, marketing or an open question.

m3-p02audioThe skin keeps its own fat.
Most people picture skin fat as one layer under the dermis. There is a second, thinner layer inside the reticular dermis itself, called dermal white adipose tissue. In mice, this layer expands rapidly after a Staphylococcus aureus infection, and its new fat cells release cathelicidin, an antimicrobial peptide. Older mice lose much of that capacity, through TGF-beta signalling. Human skin arranges this fat differently, in small cones around hair follicles, and human acne lesions contain preadipocytes around the follicle that make cathelicidin. What the layer contributes to human skin ageing has not yet been measured. So hold two things apart when you hear about skin fat. The defence story is strong in mice and emerging in people. The ageing story in people is still a proposal.

m3-p03stat
186 pairs of identical twins compared for facial ageing		Fat that holds the face up.
Cadaver dissection shows that the face's subcutaneous fat sits in separate compartments, and facial ageing is partly how each compartment changes. Weight shows the same thing from the outside. In a study of 186 pairs of identical twins, the twin with the higher body-mass index looked older before about age 40 and younger after it.
The fat compartments of the face: anatomy and clinical implications for cosmetic surgery

m3-p04audioSugar that sticks to collagen.
Glycation is chemistry before it is biology. Reducing sugars attach to the amino groups of proteins, rearrange into more stable products, and end as advanced glycation end products, known as AGEs. Dermal collagen lasts for years, so it collects AGEs, and they form cross-links that make the matrix stiffer. AGEs also bind a receptor called RAGE, which in laboratory studies switches on inflammatory signalling and enzymes that break down matrix. Keep the registers apart here. The chemistry, and the build-up of AGEs in human skin, are well documented. The idea that AGEs cause the wrinkles and laxity you see in clinic rests on laboratory and animal work plus human associations. The Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide rates the structural and inflammatory effects grade B. This course grades the causal link D, and the next moment shows why.
Advanced glycation end products: Key players in skin aging?
Accumulation of Maillard reaction products in skin collagen in diabetes and aging
Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m3-p05stat
~15 years estimated half-life of human skin collagen		Fifteen years on the same collagen.
Human skin collagen turns over slowly; tissue analysis puts its half-life at about 15 years. In skin biopsies, glycoxidation products in collagen rose about five-fold between ages 20 and 85, and were up to twice as high in type 1 diabetes. Skin autofluorescence, a non-invasive reading, correlates moderately with those biopsy measures and was validated in non-pigmented skin.

Lesson 3.1
m3-p08audioTwo gut hormones, re-engineered.
After a meal, the gut releases two hormones called incretins, GLP-1 and GIP. Both amplify insulin release when glucose is high, and GLP-1 also slows the stomach and reduces appetite. Native GLP-1 lasts only minutes in the blood before an enzyme breaks it down, so the drug molecules are engineered to last far longer. Semaglutide activates the GLP-1 receptor. Tirzepatide activates both the GIP and the GLP-1 receptors. Those are the mechanisms. The regulatory facts sit in a separate register. In the United States, as of September 2026, both molecules are FDA-approved drugs, sold under one brand name for type 2 diabetes and another for chronic weight management, with further indications for the heart, the liver and sleep apnoea. The face appears nowhere on those labels, and that is where this lesson begins.
Mechanisms of Action and Therapeutic Application of Glucagon-like Peptide-1

m3-p10stat
14.9% · 20.9% mean weight loss in STEP-1 (semaglutide, 68 weeks) and SURMOUNT-1 (tirzepatide, highest dose, 72 weeks)		Large trials, measured on the scale.

m3-p12audioCounsel before, record during, refer when asked.
Three moments matter in practice. Before treatment starts, raise facial volume change and hair shedding as possibilities, especially with older patients, as the Hormonaly guide advises, and record that you did. During treatment, photograph the face with consent so that change is measured rather than remembered, and support the protein intake and resistance training the guide recommends to protect lean mass. When a patient asks for help with the face, refer to a qualified aesthetic or dermatology colleague. The published advice on restoring volume after drug-driven weight loss is expert opinion, the main consensus statement was developed with industry involvement, and randomized trials of fillers and devices in these patients are registered with no results yet. Whatever your role, decisions about the medicine itself stay with the prescriber and the patient.

m3-p13audioTen months in, and unhappy with the mirror.
Picture a fifty-eight-year-old patient, fictional and built from this lesson's evidence. She has taken semaglutide for obesity for ten months, prescribed by her physician, and has lost eighteen percent of her body weight. Her blood pressure and glucose have improved. She is distressed by hollow cheeks and new looseness along the jaw, and she is finding more hair in her brush. She asks whether she should stop the injections. Consider what the evidence says. In the studies so far, facial volume loss tracks the amount of weight lost. Hair loss is a labelled side effect, reported more often in women and linked to weight reduction. The drug was prescribed for a medical reason, and that reason is going well. Decide how you would answer her.

Lesson 3.2
m3-p15audioInflammation that comes with age.
Ageing brings a slow rise in background inflammation, called inflammaging. It is low-grade and sterile, and in older adults higher IL-6 and C-reactive protein predict frailty, disability and death. One proposed source is the gut. In mice, a high-fat diet raises bacterial lipopolysaccharide in the blood, and infusing that endotoxin reproduces weight gain and insulin resistance. In twelve healthy men, a single high-fat meal raised plasma endotoxin within four hours. That is the mechanism usually called metabolic endotoxaemia, or leaky gut. The human evidence stops short of the skin. Measuring gut permeability is difficult, and a leading review concludes that it is still unproven whether restoring the barrier improves disease outside the gut. Hold that line as you look at what patients are sold.
Inflamm-aging. An evolutionary perspective on immunosenescence
Inflammageing: chronic inflammation in ageing, cardiovascular disease, and frailty
Metabolic endotoxemia initiates obesity and insulin resistance
A high-fat meal induces low-grade endotoxemia: evidence of a novel mechanism of postprandial inflammation
Leaky gut: mechanisms, measurement and clinical implications in humans

m3-p16stat
39 RCTs in the Cochrane review of oral probiotics for treating eczema		Thirty-nine trials of probiotics for eczema.
An independent Cochrane review pooled 39 randomized trials, with 2,599 participants, of oral probiotics for eczema. Probiotics probably make little or no difference to patient-rated symptoms, and investigator-rated severity fell by less than the minimum clinically important difference. A 2024 European task-force meta-analysis in children without food allergy reported a significant fall in severity scores.

m3-p18audioDiet first, a bottle last.
Patients often link their skin to their gut. Start where the evidence is. Diet quality, treatment of metabolic disease and the established treatment for the skin condition itself all come first. Be careful with tests. A widely used commercial zonulin assay was shown to detect proteins other than zonulin, so a result cannot tell you whether a gut is leaky. Be careful with products too. Probiotics are strain-specific, and their effects on eczema are small at best. The Hormonaly guide gives the popular gut-repair stack of glutamine, zinc carnosine and BPC-157 a grade of D. As of September 2026, in the United States, BPC-157 remains off the 503A bulks list after the July advisory-committee review. Investigational peptides come last, if at all. Whatever your role, name the evidence type before you name the product.

m4-p02reflectionHow will you use this module?

m4-p03audioA plasma tripeptide with a copper habit.
Start with the molecule. GHK is a three-amino-acid peptide that circulates in human plasma; it was first described in 1973. It binds copper readily, and the copper complex, GHK-Cu, is what skin-care labels mean when they say copper peptide. The proposed mechanism is that GHK-Cu delivers copper to enzymes that build and protect the dermal matrix, such as lysyl oxidase and superoxide dismutase, and shifts gene expression toward wound healing. Two things to hold on to. First, this is a mechanism, which explains why the peptide might work and leaves open whether it does. Second, most of this literature was written by the group that discovered the peptide. Keep both in mind when you reach the trials.

m4-p06audioSerum yes, syringe no.
Here is what that means in the room. Topical GHK-Cu is a reasonable cosmetic conversation, as long as you describe the evidence honestly: small trials, modest effects, grade C. Injected GHK-Cu is not the same product in a stronger form. There are no randomized trials of injection, injection-site pain and rash are common because copper irritates tissue, and in the United States the injectable route sits outside the Category 1 listing that covers non-injectable use. Whatever your role, name the route every time you talk about this peptide.

m4-p08audioThree families, one question: who ran the trial?
Lesson two covers three families that fill the cosmetic and supplement shelves. Signal peptides, like palmitoyl pentapeptide-4, sold as Matrixyl, are fragments of matrix proteins meant to tell fibroblasts to make more collagen. SNAP-25 mimics, Argireline and its longer cousin SNAP-8, copy part of a nerve-terminal protein that botulinum toxin targets; the idea is a gentle, topical version of that effect. And oral collagen peptides are swallowed, not applied. Every one of these has trials behind it. As you go through them, keep asking one question before any other: who ran the trial, and who paid for it.

m4-p11audioCosmetic, supplement, drug: three rulebooks.
Now the regulatory register, stated for the United States. Cosmetic peptides are cosmetic ingredients, not drugs; their safety is assessed under a cosmetics framework, and nobody has to prove they work before they are sold. Oral collagen is sold as a dietary supplement, so it cannot claim to treat a disease, and what is in the tub varies from brand to brand. That is the whole reason the funding question matters here. When no regulator asks for efficacy, the only efficacy data are the trials someone chose to pay for.

Lesson 4.2
m4-p13audioRodents, a fragment, and a tripeptide.
Lesson three moves from the cosmetic shelf to the peptides patients buy online and inject. BPC-157 is a fifteen-residue peptide first described in 1993. Its evidence is a large rodent literature, most of it from one research group, and uncontrolled human case series. TB-500 is sold as thymosin, but when anti-doping chemists analysed the product they found a seven-residue fragment, residues seventeen to twenty-three of thymosin beta-four. That fragment has never been tested in a trial. KPV is the last three amino acids of alpha-MSH; it calms inflammation in mouse colitis models, and there is no randomized human trial of it for anything. All three have plausible mechanisms, and none has a controlled human trial.

0 randomized efficacy trials of BPC-157 in people published in full (September 2026)		One abstract, no published trial.

m5-p02audioA follicle that runs its own hormones.
Each hair follicle behaves like a small gland. When researchers keep human scalp follicles alive in culture, the follicles answer corticotropin-releasing hormone the way the pituitary and adrenal glands do. They make ACTH and alpha-MSH, they secrete cortisol, and cortisol-like signals feed back to switch the stimulus down. The same follicles run a growth hormone and IGF-1 loop of their own. On the scalp, an enzyme called five-alpha-reductase turns testosterone into dihydrotestosterone, and in genetically susceptible follicles that hormone drives the gradual shrinking behind pattern hair loss. Growing follicles also hide from the immune system. When that protection collapses, immune cells attack the hair bulb, and the result is alopecia areata. Most of this biology comes from tissue kept alive outside the body, so hold it as mechanism, a reason something might matter in patients.
Human hair follicles display a functional equivalent of the hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal axis and synthesize cortisol
Human Hair Follicles Operate a Functional Peripheral Equivalent of the Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Somatotropic Axis Ex Vivo
Hair follicle immune privilege and its collapse in alopecia areata
The effectiveness of treatments for androgenetic alopecia: A systematic review and meta-analysis

m5-p03stat
1,200 adults with severe alopecia areata in the two phase 3 trials of baricitinib (2022)		Two trials, 1,200 adults, one approval.
When immune privilege fails, the attack depends on cytokine signals that JAK inhibitors interrupt. Two randomized phase 3 trials tested oral baricitinib in 1,200 adults with severe alopecia areata. In the US, FDA approved baricitinib for adults with severe alopecia areata on 13 June 2022. The Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology guide grades it A.

m5-p05audioWork up the hair loss before anyone mentions a peptide.
Most requests for a hair peptide skip the diagnosis. Start with the pattern, ask about a trigger, look for patches and nail pitting, and look for signs of androgen excess. Check ferritin and thyroid function every time, and send a scarring pattern for biopsy. The approved options already carry strong evidence. Pooled randomized trials support topical minoxidil in men and women and finasteride in men. In the United States minoxidil is sold over the counter, while finasteride for hair loss needs a prescription. Severe or fast-spreading alopecia areata belongs with a dermatologist, where a JAK inhibitor is an approved option for adults. Whatever your role, the workup comes before the product.

Lesson 5.1
m5-p07audioFive receptors, one family of hormones.
Pigment starts with hormones the skin makes for itself. Skin cells produce a precursor called POMC and cut it into ACTH, alpha-MSH and beta-endorphin, and the same cells carry the receptors that answer them. There are five melanocortin receptors. MC1R sets the pigment of skin and hair. MC2R is the adrenal receptor for ACTH. MC3R and MC4R work in the brain on appetite, and central receptors also drive sexual responses. MC5R governs sebum. Researchers caution that the real map is messier, with receptors turning up in more tissues than the textbook suggests. For this lesson one idea matters most. A drug that stays on MC1R mostly changes pigment, and a drug that reaches the brain's receptors brings other effects with it.
Neuroendocrinology of the skin
The Multifaceted Melanocortin Receptors
An overview of benefits and risks of chronic melanocortin-1 receptor activation

In the US phase 3 trial, adults on afamelanotide spent a median 69.4 hours in direct sunlight without pain over six months, against 40.8 hours on placebo. The European trial also showed more pain-free time and fewer phototoxic reactions. Both trials were funded by the manufacturer.

m5-p10audioWhat the case reports show.
Now the harms, and the kind of evidence behind them. Everything here comes from case reports and reviews, which tell you what can happen and leave open how often it does. In the skin, existing moles darken and grow, new moles erupt in crops, some of them atypical under the microscope, and dark bands can appear in the nails. At least five melanomas have been reported during or after Melanotan use. Each patient had other risk factors, such as fair skin, sunbed use or a family history, so causation is unproven, and the concern rests on a drug that pushes melanocytes to multiply. Beyond the skin, reports describe priapism that needed surgery, a stimulant-like toxic reaction with muscle breakdown and kidney injury, and a brain swelling syndrome that FDA lists among the published serious events.

m5-p11audioNew moles after a summer of tanning shots.
Her booking says skin quality. This patient is fictional, built from the lesson's evidence. m5-p14audioA Wnt switch and a zinc question.
Two peptides are sold for hair, and both arrive with better stories than data. PTD-DBM joins a cell-penetrating fragment borrowed from an HIV protein to a short decoy sequence. Inside follicle cells, the decoy keeps a protein called CXXC5 from binding its partner, Dishevelled, and that releases a brake on Wnt signalling, a pathway involved in hair regrowth. In mice, hair regrew faster and follicles formed in healed wounds. The work came from the developer's own group. Zinc thymulin is a thymic hormone that becomes active only when zinc binds to it. How it might help hair has never been defined, and nobody has shown whether any effect would come from the peptide or from the zinc. Hold both as mechanism, and look for the human trial.

Contribution of zinc and other metals to the biological activity of the serum thymic factor

m5-p17audioWhen the request is a compounded hair spray.
Stated for the United States, the regulatory picture is short. On FDA's category lists of the fourteenth of May, twenty twenty-six, thymulin acetate sits in Category 3, meaning it was nominated without enough information for FDA to evaluate, and it falls outside the enforcement discretion that Category 1 substances receive. PTD-DBM appears on none of the three lists. Neither substance is on the 503A bulks list itself. The evidence grade for both is D. So when a patient asks for a compounded hair spray, give the grade plainly, go back to the diagnosis, and describe the approved options and what they can offer.

M06 The growth-hormone axis and the skin · The growth-hormone axis and the skin · 30 min · 20 blocks

Objective. By the end, you can analyze the impact of growth hormone and IGF-1 axis modulation on dermal architecture, collagen homeostasis, fluid dynamics, and mitogenic safety concerns.

Agent: [verify] Examines growth hormone and IGF-1 dermal action, the somatopause, clinical trial data in healthy aging, tesamorelin trials, secretagogue compounding status, and acromegaly/cancer risks. Thorough and balanced.

BlockTypeWhat the learner hears / readsEvidence
m6-p01titleReal endocrinology, borrowed for marketing.
Growth hormone and IGF-1 genuinely shape skin, and one peptide on this axis has approval-grade trials. Most of what patients are offered has much less behind it. In three lessons you will separate the physiology from the promise, the approved from the anecdotal, and the side-effects you can see from the risks nobody has measured.
—

Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology — A Clinician's Pocket Guide
m6-p02audioTwo hormones, two layers of skin.
Two layers of skin listen to two different parts of the same axis. Growth hormone speaks mainly to the dermis. Its receptor is expressed more on fibroblasts than on keratinocytes, and in culture growth hormone increases fibroblast numbers and supports collagen synthesis, sometimes directly and sometimes through IGF-1. The epidermis listens to IGF-1. Keratinocytes carry the IGF-1 receptor, their neighbours in the dermis supply the hormone, and in culture IGF-1 makes keratinocytes divide where growth hormone on its own does nothing. Human hair follicles even keep a small growth-hormone loop running when they are cultured outside the body. With age, growth-hormone pulses and IGF-1 fall steadily, a change called the somatopause. Hold all of this as mechanism. It explains why the axis could matter for skin. Whether changing it helps a patient is a separate question, and the trials answer it.
Expression of growth hormone receptor, insulin-like growth factor 1 (IGF-1) and IGF-1 receptor mRNA and proteins in human skin
Epidermal homeostasis: the role of the growth hormone and insulin-like growth factor systems

m6-p03stat
7.1% rise in skin thickness in 12 older men given growth hormone for six months (1990)		A much-cited skin result, read again.
In 1990 a much-cited New England Journal of Medicine study gave growth hormone for six months to 12 healthy men over 60, with nine untreated men for comparison and no placebo. Lean mass rose and fat fell. Skin thickness rose 7.1%, with P = 0.07, short of statistical significance. A small 1996 randomized trial in 28 older men found 15% more collagen laid down in implanted wound tubes on GH, a laboratory surrogate for repair.
Effects of human growth hormone in men over 60 years old
Effect of growth hormone replacement on wound healing in healthy older men

m6-p05audioDeficiency is a diagnosis. Decline is ageing.
Here the physiology meets the waiting room. A patient in their fifties has read that growth hormone falls with age and asks whether topping it up would help their skin. The trials in healthy older adults answer that. Pooled, they show about two kilograms less fat and two kilograms more lean mass, with more swelling, joint pain, carpal tunnel symptoms and a drift towards diabetes, while strength and stamina change little. Graded on those trials the evidence is B, consistent and independent though small, and the Aesthetic and Regenerative Endocrinology guide gives its advice against treatment an A. Both land in the same place. Growth hormone treats a diagnosed deficiency and a short list of approved conditions. In the United States, distributing it for anti-ageing is a federal offence. Whatever your role, name the decline for what it is, a normal part of ageing.
Growth hormone and sex steroid administration in healthy aged women and men: a randomized controlled trial

Lesson 6.1
m6-p07audioOne axis, four ways in.
Four routes lead into the same axis, and patients rarely know which one they are on. Somatropin is growth hormone itself, made by recombinant technology. GHRH analogues, sermorelin, tesamorelin and CJC-1295, press on the pituitary's releasing-hormone receptor, so the gland releases its own growth hormone in pulses. Ghrelin-receptor agonists, ipamorelin, GHRP-2, GHRP-6 and the oral compound ibutamoren, press on a second pituitary receptor. IGF-1 analogues such as IGF-1 LR3 skip the pituitary and act on the IGF-1 receptor directly. Every route ends at that same receptor, which is why lesson three treats their risks together. Clinics often sell all of these as one family with one promise. Keep them apart, because the trials, the approvals and the side-effects belong to each molecule separately.

m6-p12audioName the indication before the molecule.
Imagine the request as it usually arrives. A patient says their clinic offers a peptide that raises growth hormone naturally, for skin and sleep, and asks what you think. Three answers come before anything else. First, which molecule, exactly, because CJC-1295 with and without its albumin-binding tail are two different products, and only the version with the tail has been tested in people. Second, what stands behind it. Tesamorelin has approval-grade trials for visceral fat in people with HIV, and the rest stop at hormone levels or have never been studied in humans. Third, where it stands in the United States today, read off FDA's live lists. As of September 2026, none of the growth-hormone secretagogues sits on the 503A bulks list.

Lesson 6.2
m6-p14audioWhat too much growth hormone looks like.
Excess growth hormone leaves visible marks on skin and soft tissue, and noticing them early matters. In acromegaly, glycosaminoglycan deposits and oedema thicken the face, hands and feet, so features look puffy and coarse. The skin turns oily with large pores, sweating increases, and skin tags and acanthosis nigricans appear. Hands and feet enlarge slowly, and the jaw widens. Drugs on this axis bring their own signs, usually transient, with swelling, joint pain, tingling fingers and carpal tunnel symptoms. On somatropin, the label asks for existing moles to be watched for growth or malignant change. The Endocrine Society recommends measuring IGF-1 when typical acral and facial features are present, and suggests it when several associated conditions cluster. For most of you the job is recognition and referral, and the diagnosis belongs to endocrinology.
Skin manifestations in acromegaly

Consensus on criteria for acromegaly diagnosis and remission

47% of tesamorelin-treated patients with IGF-1 above +2 SD at 26 weeks in its pivotal trials		Half the patients on the approved one.

Its label states that the effects of prolonged IGF-1 elevation are unknown. For unapproved secretagogues bought for skin, nobody has published the equivalent figure.

· Tesamorelin raises growth hormone through the pituitary, so its GRADE A covers skin ageing.

M07 Evidence, regulation and compliance · Evidence, regulation and compliance · 33 min · 23 blocks

Objective. By the end, you can apply statutory drug compounding frameworks, quality assurance standards, and GRADE criteria to establish compliant, evidence-graded clinical practices in aesthetic endocrinology.

Agent: [verify] Synthesizes evidence grading (GRADE), US compounding laws (503A/503B), certificate of analysis auditing, international unlicensed medicine pathways, and chart documentation. Crucial compliance capstone.

BlockTypeWhat the learner hears / readsEvidence
m7-p01titleGrade it, check it, write it down.
This last module turns the course into three habits. You will rate the certainty behind a claim with GRADE, read a compounding status and a certificate of analysis for what they do and do not prove, and write the consent and chart note that an unapproved product needs. The regulatory detail is given for the US in the lesson text, and for the UK, the EU, Portugal, Brazil and the UAE in each moment's regulatory note; lesson 7.2 covers the routes for unlicensed medicines and the rules on selling and advertising peptides outside the US.

m7-p02audioGRADE rates certainty in an effect, one outcome at a time.
Every claim you hear about a skin peptide carries a hidden question. How sure can we be? GRADE answers it with four levels of certainty, from high to very low. The rating belongs to a body of evidence for one outcome, such as wrinkle depth at twelve weeks, and never to a single study or to the molecule itself. Randomized trials start high and observational studies start low. From there, certainty drops for five reasons you can name, which are risk of bias, inconsistency between trials, indirectness, imprecision, and suspected publication bias. It can occasionally rise, for example when an effect is very large. Both Hormonaly guides print a letter from A to D beside each use. Read those letters as the same four levels, adapted. They rate the evidence for that use, and they say nothing about whether a product is legal.
GRADE guidelines: 3. Rating the quality of evidence
GRADE: an emerging consensus on rating quality of evidence and strength of recommendations

m7-p03stat
1.27 risk ratio for a favourable efficacy result, industry-sponsored versus other studies		Sponsored studies come out better.
A Cochrane methodology review brought together 75 papers, each comparing studies sponsored by the manufacturer with studies funded in other ways. Industry-sponsored drug and device studies more often reported favourable efficacy results and favourable conclusions, and the difference was not explained by the usual risk-of-bias checks. It is the pattern you met with oral collagen in module 04.
Industry sponsorship and research outcome
Effects of Collagen Supplements on Skin Aging: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials
Core GRADE 4: rating certainty of evidence—risk of bias, publication bias, and reasons for rating up certainty
m7-p04toolName what lowers certainty.
GRADE names the ways a body of evidence can mislead. Read the four you will use most. Then sort four cases from this course by the reason that most lowers certainty. The fifth, publication bias, is suspected when the evidence is a handful of small, mostly commercially funded studies.

m7-p05audioTwo checks, never one.
Suppose a patient asks whether a peptide works. Start with the outcome they care about, say fewer lines around the eyes, and give the certainty for that outcome, along with who paid for the trials behind it. Then, as a separate step, check what the product legally is. The Hormonaly guides keep these two checks apart for a reason. An approved drug can carry a low grade for the particular use a patient has in mind, and a compound with stronger human data may still have no lawful route in the United States. A persuasive mechanism leaves the grade exactly where it is. Whatever your role, say the outcome and the grade in one breath, and treat the regulatory answer as a question of its own.

Lesson 7.1
m7-p07audioTwo compounding routes, one strict order.
Two routes exist for compounding in the United States, and the difference between them shapes the rest of this lesson. Under section 503A, a licensed pharmacist or physician prepares a drug for an identified patient on a prescription. If the conditions are met, the product escapes three requirements, namely approval as a new drug, labelling with adequate directions, and good manufacturing practice. The conditions start with the substance itself, taken in strict order. A USP or National Formulary monograph comes first. Only when there is none can it be a component of an approved drug, and only when neither applies can it sit on FDA's 503A bulks list. The substance must also come from a registered manufacturer with a valid certificate of analysis. Section 503B outsourcing facilities work differently. They register with FDA, work to good manufacturing practice, face risk-based inspection, and can compound without a prescription for each patient. Their products remain unapproved.
21 U.S.C. § 353a — Pharmacy compounding (FD&C Act section 503A)
Bulk Drug Substances Used in Compounding Under Section 503A of the FD&C Act
21 U.S.C. § 353b — Outsourcing facilities (FD&C Act section 503B)
Information for Outsourcing Facilities

m7-p09stat
40 amino acids: above this, FDA treats the molecule as a protein, and so as a biological product		Forty amino acids.
Since 23 March 2020 FDA defines a protein as an alpha amino acid polymer of more than 40 amino acids, whether or not it is chemically synthesized, so anything above the line is a biological product. On that date tesamorelin, a 44-residue GHRH analogue, and the enzyme hyaluronidase moved to biologics licences, and FDA told compounders that transitioned biologics cannot use the 503A or 503B exemptions.
EGRIFTA WR (tesamorelin) for injection — prescribing information

m7-p21toolOutside the US, 503A does not apply.
FDA's 503A bulks list and its categories are US law and policy and apply only in the US. In the other jurisdictions in this course, a medicine without a marketing authorisation reaches a patient as a pharmacy preparation or an unlicensed supply made on a prescription, and the prescriber answers for that choice.

m7-p10audioWhat a certificate can and cannot prove.
Picture the certificate a patient hands you, and read it line by line, asking what each line actually tests. Mass spectrometry checks identity, and a careful certificate shows the expected mass next to the one measured. HPLC reports purity as a percentage of the main peak, which tells you how much else is present and nothing about what that something else is. Endotoxin matters for anything injected, and FDA's guidance says the acceptable limit is calculated from the dose and the route, so a bare figure settles little. When FDA reviewed BPC-157 for its advisory committee, most public certificates listed purity alone, and the one a nominator supplied mentioned no endotoxin test. Then remember what paper cannot do. A certificate describes a lot. It cannot show that your vial came from that lot, how the vial was stored, or that it is sterile.

m7-p12stat
7.7–14.4% measured purity of online semaglutide vials labelled 99%		99% on the label.
Researchers made test purchases of semaglutide from illegal online pharmacies and analysed what arrived. The three vials that were delivered were all judged probably substandard or falsified: purity measured 7.7–14.4% against the 99% printed on the label, peptide content ran 29–39% above the labelled amount, and endotoxin was found in every vial, although none grew microorganisms.
Multifactor Quality and Safety Analysis of Semaglutide Products Sold by Online Sellers Without a Prescription: Market Surveillance, Content Analysis, and Product Purchase Evaluation Study

m7-p13audioResearch use only is a label, and never a route.
Research use only sounds like a category. In law it works as a shipping permission. The US rule lets a supplier send an investigational drug for laboratory or test-tube work if the label says it must stay out of people, and the shipper is meant to confirm that is how it will be used. FDA judges what a product is for by how it is marketed and sold, so a disclaimer on a vial offered with injection instructions carries no weight. The Hormonaly guides say it plainly. Research use only is not a clinical pathway. A certificate of analysis, however clean, cannot convert that vial into a pharmaceutical, because sterility, manufacturing standards and a lawful route are all still missing. Whatever your role, treat these products as outside clinical care, and write down exactly what you were told.
21 CFR 312.160 — Drugs for investigational use in laboratory research animals or in vitro tests
21 CFR 201.128 — Meaning of "intended uses"

m7-p22audioSelling and advertising peptides outside the US.
Selling a peptide outside the United States starts with one question, whether the product is a medicine, and regulators answer it from the product itself. They look at what is claimed for it and at what it is and does, so a research-use label or a cosmetic box settles nothing. A medicine without a marketing authorisation may not be sold, supplied or advertised. Advertising a prescription-only medicine to the public is prohibited, or needs the regulator's approval first, and that covers posts on social media by clinics and influencers. The details differ from country to country, so check the rules for your own jurisdiction, shown with this moment, and date what you find.
A guide to what is a medicinal product — MHRA Guidance Note 8
The Human Medicines Regulations 2012 (SI 2012/1916), regulation 46 — Requirement for authorisation
The Human Medicines Regulations 2012 (SI 2012/1916), regulation 279 — Products without a marketing authorisation etc
The Human Medicines Regulations 2012 (SI 2012/1916), regulation 284 — Prescription only medicines (advertising)

Lesson 7.2
m7-p15audioOff-label needs a label.
Off-label is the phrase to get right first. FDA uses it for an approved drug prescribed outside its labelling, which prescribers generally may do when they judge it medically appropriate. The idea assumes a label exists. A peptide that was never approved has none, so prescribing it counts as using an unapproved drug, and the Peptide Pocket Guide says exactly that. Compounding leaves that answer unchanged. A compounded product is unapproved by definition, and it is lawful only when the substance and the pharmacy meet the statute's conditions. Under federal law, putting an unapproved new drug into interstate commerce is a prohibited act, and in 2020 FDA warned a compounding pharmacy about its BPC-157 and GHK-Cu products on that basis. So write the real category in the chart, every time.
Understanding Unapproved Use of Approved Drugs "Off Label"
21 U.S.C. § 331 — Prohibited acts
Warning Letter — Tailor Made Compounding LLC (April 1, 2020)

m7-p17audio'Just prescribe it off-label.'
A fictional patient, built from this module's sources, is fifty-two and three weeks past a fractional laser treatment. She asks you to prescribe compounded BPC-157 injections off-label to speed her healing. A pharmacy is willing to make it, and she shows you its certificate of analysis. Three facts shape your answer. BPC-157 has never been approved, so there is no label to prescribe around. It has no 503A route, its nominations were withdrawn, and FDA's own reviewers advised against listing it in July 2026, noting a lack of evidence that it works even for the condition they studied. And a certificate describes a batch, which leaves sterility and legality exactly where they were. Decide how you will answer her.
